<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

A $280 \ \text{g}$ packet of mouse food costs $\pounds 2.50$

Work out the cost of feeding Squeak for $2$ years.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{number of weeks}                  &:& \text{cost of food} \\\\
= & 8                                       &:& \pounds 2.5 \\\\
= & 1                                       &:& \dfrac {2.5} {8} \\\\
= & 52 \times 2                             &:& \dfrac {2.5 \times 2 \times 52} {8} \\\\
= & 104                                     &:& \dfrac {25 \times 2 \times 52} {8 \times 10} \\\\
= & 104                                     &:& \dfrac {25 \times 2 \times 4 \times 13} {4 \times 2 \times 10} \\\\
= & 104                                     &:& \dfrac {25 \times \cancel 2 \times \cancel 4 \times 13} {\cancel 4 \times \cancel 2 \times 10} \\\\
= & 104                                     &:& \dfrac {25 \times 13} {10} \\\\
= & 104                                     &:& \dfrac {325} {10} \\\\
= & 104                                     &:& \pounds 32.5
\end{array}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 32.5$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Using ratio,

$

= & 52 \times 2                             &:& \dfrac {2.5 \times 2 \times 52} {8} \\\\
= & 104                                     &:& \dfrac {25 \times 2 \times 52} {8 \times 10} \\\\
= & 104                                     &:& \dfrac {25 \times 2 \times 4 \times 13} {4 \times 2 \times 10} \\\\
= & 104                                     &:& \dfrac {25 \times \cancel 2 \times \cancel 4 \times 13} {\cancel 4 \times \cancel 2 \times 10} \\\\
= & 104                                     &:& \dfrac {25 \times 13} {10} \\\\
= & 104                                     &:& \dfrac {325} {10} \\\\
= & 104                                     &:& \pounds 32.5
\end{array}
$
</code></pre>
</div>

<div class='answers'>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Squeak ‘squeaks’ once every $15 \ \text{seconds}$.

He squeaks once at $07{:}30$

How many times will Squeak ‘squeak’ between $07{:}30$ when Morag gets up,and $21{:}30$ when she goes to bed?

*Give your answer to the nearest $500$*

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of times Squeak will squeak}         &= \dfrac {21:30 - 7:30} {15} \\\\
                                                  &= \dfrac {14 \times 60 \times 60} {15} \\\\
                                                  &= \dfrac {14 \times 60 \times 15 \times 4} {15} \\\\
                                                  &= \dfrac {14 \times 60 \times \cancel {15} \times 4} {\cancel {15}} \\\\
                                                  &= 14 \times 60 \times 4 \\\\
                                                  &= 3360
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$3360$

</div>

<div class='workingcodeblock'>

                                                  &= \dfrac {14 \times 60 \times 60} {15} \\\\
                                                  &= \dfrac {14 \times 60 \times 15 \times 4} {15} \\\\
                                                  &= \dfrac {14 \times 60 \times \cancel {15} \times 4} {\cancel {15}} \\\\
                                                  &= 14 \times 60 \times 4 \\\\
                                                  &= 3360
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>

<div class='answercodeblock'>

<p>2A0FA070-25B8-4F23-B272-463207966E46</p>
</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2014--group-2:1:21</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>Md Rameez</p>

<div class='rag'>

tratio
</li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class='question question'>

Mr Cooper’s car uses $3 \ \text{litres}$ of petrol to travel $50 \ \text{kilometres}$.

Petrol costs $\pounds 1.45$ per litre.

How much will Mr Cooper spend on petrol to drive $600 \ \text{kilometres}$ from Oxford to Glasgow?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Petrol needed}     &= 600 \times \dfrac {3} {50} \\\\
                          &= \dfrac {3 \times 600} {50} \\\\
                          &= \dfrac {3 \times 50 \times 12} {50} \\\\
                          &= \dfrac {3 \times \cancel {50} \times 12} {\cancel {50}} \\\\
                          &= 3 \times 12 \\\\
                          &= 36 \ \text{litres} \\\\
\text {Total cost}        &= 1.45 \times 36 \\\\
                          &= \pounds 52.2
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{km}                                     &:& \text{litres} \\\\
= & 50                                            &:& 3 \\\\
= & 1                                             &:& \dfrac {3} {50} \\\\
= & 1 \times 600                                  &:& \dfrac {3 \times 600} {50} \\\\
= & 600                                           &:& \dfrac {3 \times 50 \times 12} {50} \\\\
= & 600                                           &:& \dfrac {3 \times \cancel {50} \times 12} {\cancel {50}} \\\\
= & 600                                           &:& 3 \times 12 \\\\
= & 600 \ \text{km}                               &:& 36 \ \text{litres}
\end{array}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Total cost}        &= 1.45 \times 36 \\\\
                          &= \pounds 52.2
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 52.2$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Petrol needed}     &= 600 \times \dfrac {3} {50} \\\\
                          &= \dfrac {3 \times 600} {50} \\\\
                          &= \dfrac {3 \times 50 \times 12} {50} \\\\
                          &= \dfrac {3 \times \cancel {50} \times 12} {\cancel {50}} \\\\
                          &= 3 \times 12 \\\\
                          &= 36 \ \text{litres} \\\\
\text {Total cost}        &= 1.45 \times 36 \\\\
                          &= \pounds 52.2
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Using ratio,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{km}                                     &:& \text{litres} \\\\
= & 50                                            &:& 3 \\\\
= & 1                                             &:& \dfrac {3} {50} \\\\
= & 1 \times 600                                  &:& \dfrac {3 \times 600} {50} \\\\
= & 600                                           &:& \dfrac {3 \times 50 \times 12} {50} \\\\
= & 600                                           &:& \dfrac {3 \times \cancel {50} \times 12} {\cancel {50}} \\\\
= & 600                                           &:& 3 \times 12 \\\\
= & 600 \ \text{km}                               &:& 36 \ \text{litres}
\end{array}
$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Total cost}        &= 1.45 \times 36 \\\\
                          &= \pounds 52.2
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>

<div class='workings'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of 20 ml portions}       &= \dfrac {540} {20} \\\\
                                      &= \dfrac {20 \times 27} {20} \\\\
                                      &= \dfrac {\cancel {20} \times 27} {\cancel {20}} \\\\
                                      &= 27
\end{aligned}
$

= & 10                                      &:& \dfrac {\cancel 4 \times 5 \times 10} {\cancel 4} \\\\
= & 10                                      &:& 5 \times 10 \\\\

Maria uses $6 \ \text{litres}$ of water every time she cleans her kitchen.

She cleans her kitchen every day except for Sunday.

For how many weeks does a bottle of *Kleeno* last her?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}

= & 1                                       &:& \dfrac {20} {4} \\\\
= & 6                                       &:& \dfrac {20 \times 6} {4} \\\\
= & 6                                       &:& \dfrac {4 \times 5 \times 6} {4} \\\\
= & 6                                       &:& \dfrac {\cancel 4 \times 5 \times 6} {\cancel 4} \\\\
= & 6                                       &:& 5 \times 6 \\\\
= & 6 \ \text{litres}                       &:& 30 \ \text{ml}
\end{array}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of weeks}      &= \dfrac {540} {30 \times 6} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {30 \times 6 \times 3} {30 \times 6} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {\cancel {30} \times \cancel 6 \times 3} {\cancel {30} \times \cancel 6} \\\\
                            &= 3
\end{aligned}
$

</div>

\end{aligned}

<div class='workings'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Number of tea leaves}      &= \dfrac {1000} {0.008} \\\\
                                  &= \dfrac {1000 \times 1000} {8} \\\\
                                  &= \dfrac {1000 \times 125 \times 8} {8} \\\\
                                  &= \dfrac {1000 \times 125 \times \cancel 8} {\cancel 8} \\\\
                                  &= 1000 \times 125 \\\\
                                  &= 125000
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

                                  &= \dfrac {1000 \times 125 \times 8} {8} \\\\
                                  &= \dfrac {1000 \times 125 \times \cancel 8} {\cancel 8} \\\\
                                  &= 1000 \times 125 \\\\
                                  &= 125000
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

A tea bag contains $250$ tea leaves.

The tissue covering weighs $0.5 \ \text{g}$.

How many tea bags will there be in a $250 \text{g}$ packet of tea bags?

(The $250 \text{g}$ includes the mass of the bags and not just the tea leaves.)

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Number of tea bags}        &= \dfrac {250} {250 \times 0.008 + 0.5} \\\\
                                  &= \dfrac {250} {250 \times \dfrac {8} {1000} + 0.5} \\\\
                                  &= \dfrac {250} {\dfrac {8 \times 250} {1000} + 0.5} \\\\
                                  &= \dfrac {250} {\dfrac {2000} {1000} + 0.5} \\\\
                                  &= \dfrac {250} {\dfrac {2 \cancel {000}} {1 \cancel {000}} + 0.5} \\\\
                                  &= \dfrac {250} {2 + 0.5} \\\\
                                  &= \dfrac {250} {2.5} \\\\
                                  &= \dfrac {250 \times 10} {25} \\\\
                                  &= \dfrac {25 \times 10 \times 10} {25} \\\\
                                  &= \dfrac {\cancel {25} \times 10 \times 10} {\cancel {25}} \\\\
                                  &= 10 \times 10 \\\\
                                  &= 100
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$100 \ \text {bags}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Number of tea bags}        &= \dfrac {250} {250 \times 0.008 + 0.5} \\\\
                                  &= \dfrac {250} {250 \times \dfrac {8} {1000} + 0.5} \\\\
                                  &= \dfrac {250} {\dfrac {8 \times 250} {1000} + 0.5} \\\\
                                  &= \dfrac {250} {\dfrac {2000} {1000} + 0.5} \\\\
                                  &= \dfrac {250} {\dfrac {2 \cancel {000}} {1 \cancel {000}} + 0.5} \\\\
                                  &= \dfrac {250} {2 + 0.5} \\\\
                                  &= \dfrac {250} {2.5} \\\\
                                  &= \dfrac {250 \times 10} {25} \\\\
                                  &= \dfrac {25 \times 10 \times 10} {25} \\\\
                                  &= \dfrac {\cancel {25} \times 10 \times 10} {\cancel {25}} \\\\
                                  &= 10 \times 10 \\\\
                                  &= 100
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>

<div class='review-comments'>

<h4>Review Comments</h4>
Part A 

Start katex

1 Tea leaf &= 0.008 grams

1 x 1000 Tea leaves = 0.008 x 1000 grams

1000 Tea leaves = 8g

Change the blue answer too. 



Part B :tratio



Part C 

Lets find the weight of 1 Tea Bag



As per the question, 



Weight of 1 Tea bag &= 250 g / (No. of tea leaves x Weight of 1 leaf)  + Tissue (Weight)

                              &= (250 x  0.008g) + 0.5 g

&= 2  + 0.5

&= 2.5 g



No. of Tea bags in 250 g packet &= Total weight of packet / Weight of 1 Tea bag

= 250 /  2.5

= 25 x 10 x 10 / 25

= cancel

= 100 bags
</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_pr rag_prns question'>
<div class='uuid'>
<p>77F62AEF-602C-4E6C-8EBE-202B89E04308</p>
</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2016--group-1:1:44</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>Md Rameez</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_mr_pr</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_prns</p>
</div>
<div class='topics'>
<ul>
<li>
tratio
</li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class='question question'>

In the same time that Tom can run $100 \ \text{metres}$, Jerry can run only $60 \ \text{metres}$.

Tom and Jerry have a competition.

When Jerry has run $100 \ \text{metres}$, Tom sets off in pursuit.

How many metres will Tom run before he catches Jerry?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the distance travelled by Tom $= a$

Distance travelled by Jerry $= a + 100$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
100 \ \text{Tom} : 60 \ \text{Jerry}            &= a \ \text{Tom} : (a + 100) \ \text{Jerry} \\\\
\dfrac{100} {60}                                &= \dfrac {a} {a + 100} \\\\
100 \times (a + 100)                            &= a \times 60 \\\\
100a + 10000                                    &= 60a \\\\
100a - 60a                                      &= 10000 \\\\
40a                                             &= 10000 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {10000} {40} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {40 \times 250} {40} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel {40} \times 250} {\cancel {40}} \\\\
a                                               &= 250 \ \text{m}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$250 \ \text{m}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the distance travelled by Tom $= a$
<emptyline>
Distance travelled by Jerry $= a + 100$
<emptyline>
Using equivalent ratio,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
100 \ \text{Tom} : 60 \ \text{Jerry}            &= a \ \text{Tom} : (a + 100) \ \text{Jerry} \\\\
\dfrac{100} {60}                                &= \dfrac {a} {a + 100} \\\\
100 \times (a + 100)                            &= a \times 60 \\\\
100a + 10000                                    &= 60a \\\\
100a - 60a                                      &= 10000 \\\\
40a                                             &= 10000 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {10000} {40} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {40 \times 250} {40} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel {40} \times 250} {\cancel {40}} \\\\
a                                               &= 250 \ \text{m}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$250 \ \text{m}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

What is the mass of ‘*Salmon pasta*’ in the whole pack?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{fibre}                            &:& \text{salmon pasta} \\\\
= & 2 \ \text{g}                            &:& 100 \ \text{g} \\\\
= & 1                                       &:& \dfrac {100} {2} \\\\
= & 7                                       &:& \dfrac {100 \times 7} {2} \\\\
= & 7                                       &:& \dfrac {2 \times 50 \times 7} {2} \\\\
= & 7                                       &:& \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times 50 \times 7} {\cancel 2} \\\\
= & 7                                       &:& 50 \times 7 \\\\
= & 7 \ \text{g}                            &:& 350 \ \text{g}
\end{array}
$

</div>
</div>

$350 \ \text {g}$

<div class='question question'>

$1 \ \text{cm}$ on a map represents a distance of $2 \ \text{km}$ on the ground. What is the distance in metres represented on the ground by $3 \ \text{mm}$ on the map?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the distance on ground in metres $= a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
1 \ \text{cm map} : 2 \ \text{km ground}                        &= 3 \ \text{mm map} : a \ \text{m ground} \\\\
\dfrac {1 \ \text{cm}} {2 \ \text{km}}                          &= \dfrac {3 \ \text{mm}} {a \ \text{m}} \\\\
\dfrac {1 \times 10 \ \text{mm}} {2 \times 1000 \ \text{m}}     &= \dfrac {3 \ \text{mm}} {a \ \text{m}} \\\\
\dfrac {10} {2000}                                              &= \dfrac {3} {a} \\\\
10a                                                             &= 3 \times 2000 \\\\
a                                                               &= \dfrac {3 \times 2000} {10} \\\\
a                                                               &= \dfrac {3 \times 200 \cancel 0} {1 \cancel 0} \\\\
a                                                               &= 3 \times 200 \\\\
a                                                               &= 600 \ \text{m}
\end{aligned}
$

Distance on ground $= 600 \ \text{m}$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{map}                &:& \text{ground} \\\\
= & 1 \ \text{cm}             &:& 2 \ \text{km} \\\\
= & 1 \times 10 \ \text{mm}   &:& 2 \times 1000 \ \text{m} \\\\
= & 10                        &:& 2000 \\\\
= & 1                         &:& \dfrac{2000} {10} \\\\
= & 3                         &:& \dfrac{2000 \times 3} {10} \\\\
= & 3                         &:& \dfrac{200 \cancel 0 \times 3} {1 \cancel 0} \\\\
= & 3                         &:& 200 \times 3 \\\\
= & 3 \ \text{mm}             &:& 600 \ \text{m} \\\\
\end{array}
$

Distance on ground $= 600 \ \text{m}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$600 \ \text{m}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>
